THE GRAVITY RESTAURANT SOP
LIBRARY
VOLUME 3
Specialty, Advanced & Hard-to-Find SOPs

175 ready-to-customize standard operating procedures for specialty service models,
uncommon operations, high-risk processes, special events, multi-unit situations,
technology failures, and restaurant circumstances beyond everyday operations.

Thomas Butler
Thomas Monroe Books | The Gravity Restaurant Leadership System


Digital Edition Notice
This digital edition is available through Thomas Monroe Books. Print-retail preparation for Lulu or Amazon is a separate production
status and does not restrict access to this digital edition.

Customization and Compliance
These SOPs are operational frameworks. Customize restaurant-specific limits, authorities, equipment, vendors, forms, regulatory
requirements, and local rules before adoption. High-risk or regulated procedures should be reviewed against the requirements that
apply to the specific operation and jurisdiction.

How to Use This Volume
Use the SOP as a controlled starting point. Assign ownership, customize the fields, verify the procedure against the operation, approve
the controlled version, train affected employees, and retain revision evidence. The Gravity SOP Foundry provides the companion
customization and document-control workflow.

V3-001 | Catering, Banquets & Private Events

Catering Inquiry Intake and Qualification SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for catering inquiry intake and qualification so specialty operations do not
depend on improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Catering managers, event coordinators, banquet captains, kitchen leaders, service leaders, and employees assigned to private or
off-site events.

The event owner coordinates the procedure. The manager on duty or designated catering leader verifies readiness, exceptions,
records, and final closeout.

Whenever this specialty operation is performed and at each required monitoring, handoff, verification, or closeout point defined by the
restaurant.

• Approved event order, contract, proposal, or event brief

• Event production and staffing plan

• Approved packing, transport, setup, service, and closeout checklists

• Communication devices and manager contact list

• Required food-safety, payment, incident, and reconciliation records

• The current approved event order or written event scope controls execution.

• Food, equipment, staffing, payment, and guest commitments are verified before service begins.

• Off-site conditions are checked before product or employees are exposed to avoidable risk.

• Changes, shortages, service failures, and post-event variances are documented and handed to the accountable manager.

1. Confirm the current approved scope for catering inquiry intake and qualification, including date/time, location, guest count or demand assumption,
responsible leader, and special commitments.

2. Review staffing, production, equipment, transport/site, payment, guest-access, safety, and food-safety requirements; assign an owner to each critical
control.

3. Inspect the required area, equipment, supplies, communication method, and backup resources before product or guests arrive.

4. Stage and label materials in the sequence they will be used; separate guest-specific, event-specific, allergen, payment, and high-risk items as
applicable.

5. Execute catering inquiry intake and qualification using the current event/service plan and verify critical handoffs before proceeding to the next phase.

6. Monitor volume, staffing, timing, food condition, guest flow, and safety; record exceptions and adjust before control is lost.

7. Escalate shortages, unsafe conditions, late changes, quality failures, or guest commitments the team cannot meet to the event manager immediately.

8. Complete the guest/client or internal handoff, confirm unresolved items, and document any approved change to the original plan.

9. Reconcile product, equipment, payments, damage, waste, incidents, and outstanding follow-up; clean and restore the work/site area.

10. The accountable manager reviews the record, closes open issues, and captures lessons that should change the next comparable event.

■ Catering Inquiry Intake and Qualification was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.


■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.

• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

Applicable permit, regulatory, certification, manufacturer, event, or company requirement: ______________________

Restaurant-specific critical limit, service target, frequency, or acceptance criterion: __________________________

Record future changes in the restaurant master SOP index, retain required evidence, and retrain affected employees whenever the approved process,

The event owner coordinates the procedure. The manager on duty or designated catering leader verifies readiness, exceptions,
records, and final closeout.

For every applicable receipt, transfer, pickup, release, or custody handoff, before the product/order is accepted or released.

• Approved event order, contract, proposal, or event brief

• Event production and staffing plan

• Approved packing, transport, setup, service, and closeout checklists

• Communication devices and manager contact list

• Required food-safety, payment, incident, and reconciliation records

5. Execute catering proposal and contract handoff using the current event/service plan and verify critical handoffs before proceeding to the next phase.

6. Monitor volume, staffing, timing, food condition, guest flow, and safety; record exceptions and adjust before control is lost.

7. Escalate shortages, unsafe conditions, late changes, quality failures, or guest commitments the team cannot meet to the event manager immediately.

8. Complete the guest/client or internal handoff, confirm unresolved items, and document any approved change to the original plan.

9. Reconcile product, equipment, payments, damage, waste, incidents, and outstanding follow-up; clean and restore the work/site area.

V3-006 | Catering, Banquets & Private Events

Off-Site Hot Food Transport SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for off-site hot food transport so specialty operations do not depend on
improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Catering managers, event coordinators, banquet captains, kitchen leaders, service leaders, and employees assigned to private or
off-site events.

The event owner coordinates the procedure. The manager on duty or designated catering leader verifies readiness, exceptions,
records, and final closeout.

Whenever this specialty operation is performed and at each required monitoring, handoff, verification, or closeout point defined by the
restaurant.

• Approved event order, contract, proposal, or event brief

• Event production and staffing plan

• Approved packing, transport, setup, service, and closeout checklists

• Communication devices and manager contact list

• Required food-safety, payment, incident, and reconciliation records

• Calibrated probe thermometer and approved hot-holding/transport equipment

• The current approved event order or written event scope controls execution.

• Food, equipment, staffing, payment, and guest commitments are verified before service begins.

• Off-site conditions are checked before product or employees are exposed to avoidable risk.

• Changes, shortages, service failures, and post-event variances are documented and handed to the accountable manager.

1. Confirm the current approved scope for off-site hot food transport, including date/time, location, guest count or demand assumption, responsible
leader, and special commitments.

2. Review staffing, production, equipment, transport/site, payment, guest-access, safety, and food-safety requirements; assign an owner to each critical
control.

3. Inspect the required area, equipment, supplies, communication method, and backup resources before product or guests arrive.

4. Stage and label materials in the sequence they will be used; separate guest-specific, event-specific, allergen, payment, and high-risk items as
applicable.

5. Execute off-site hot food transport using the current event/service plan and verify critical handoffs before proceeding to the next phase.

6. Monitor volume, staffing, timing, food condition, guest flow, and safety; record exceptions and adjust before control is lost.

7. Escalate shortages, unsafe conditions, late changes, quality failures, or guest commitments the team cannot meet to the event manager immediately.

8. Complete the guest/client or internal handoff, confirm unresolved items, and document any approved change to the original plan.

9. Reconcile product, equipment, payments, damage, waste, incidents, and outstanding follow-up; clean and restore the work/site area.

10. The accountable manager reviews the record, closes open issues, and captures lessons that should change the next comparable event.

■ Off-Site Hot Food Transport was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.


■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.

■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.

• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

Applicable permit, regulatory, certification, manufacturer, event, or company requirement: ______________________

Restaurant-specific critical limit, service target, frequency, or acceptance criterion: __________________________

Record future changes in the restaurant master SOP index, retain required evidence, and retrain affected employees whenever the approved process,

V3-011 | Food Trucks, Mobile Units & Temporary Events

Mobile Unit Pre-Departure Readiness SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for mobile unit pre-departure readiness so specialty operations do not
depend on improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Mobile-unit managers, commissary teams, temporary-event leaders, cooks, service employees, and employees assigned to mobile
food operations.

The mobile-unit or event manager owns readiness. The person in charge verifies permits, utilities, food-safety controls, equipment,
records, and safe return/closeout.

Before the applicable operation, service period, event, route, batch, opening, or equipment use, and again after any material condition
changes.

• Current permits, event requirements, and approved operating plan

• Mobile-unit or booth opening/closing checklist

• Potable-water, wastewater, fuel, power, sanitation, and temperature records as applicable

• Approved food containers, labels, thermometers, sanitizer, and handwashing supplies

• Commissary loading/return log and emergency contacts

• Operate only within the scope approved by the applicable regulatory authority and property/event rules.

• Potable water, wastewater, handwashing, warewashing, power, ventilation, and food-temperature controls are verified before service.

• Food and single-service items remain protected during transport, setup, service, and return.

• Unsafe utility, fuel, sanitation, temperature, or structural conditions require service to stop until corrected or approved.

1. Confirm that mobile unit pre-departure readiness is authorized under the current mobile/temporary operating plan, permit, event rules, and commissary
relationship.

2. Verify the assigned person in charge, route/site, service window, emergency contacts, and required records for the operating period.

3. Inspect the unit/booth and all relevant utilities—potable water, wastewater, handwashing, warewashing, refrigeration/hot holding, fuel/power,
ventilation, lighting, and waste control.

4. Load or stage only protected, labeled, approved product and supplies; maintain separation of food, chemicals, waste, personal items, and
fuel/equipment hazards.

5. Perform mobile unit pre-departure readiness using the approved sequence and monitor any food temperature, water, power, sanitation, fuel, or
structural condition that can make operation unsafe.

6. Record required readings, fills, disposals, transfers, checks, or corrective actions at the restaurant-approved frequency.

7. Stop the affected activity when a critical utility, food-safety control, fuel system, sanitation control, or permit condition cannot be maintained.

8. Notify the manager/commissary/event authority of any deviation that affects service scope, product disposition, utilities, or safe operation.

9. At close or return, remove food/waste safely, clean and sanitize the unit/booth, secure fuel/power and equipment, and reconcile product and records.

10. The person in charge verifies the unit/site is safe, required records are complete, and unresolved defects are tagged for correction before next use.

■ Mobile Unit Pre-Departure Readiness was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.


■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.

■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.

• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

Applicable permit, regulatory, certification, manufacturer, event, or company requirement: ______________________

Restaurant-specific critical limit, service target, frequency, or acceptance criterion: __________________________

Record future changes in the restaurant master SOP index, retain required evidence, and retrain affected employees whenever the approved process,

To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for mobile unit wastewater tank control so specialty operations do not
depend on improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Mobile-unit managers, commissary teams, temporary-event leaders, cooks, service employees, and employees assigned to mobile
food operations.

The mobile-unit or event manager owns readiness. The person in charge verifies permits, utilities, food-safety controls, equipment,
records, and safe return/closeout.

Whenever this specialty operation is performed and at each required monitoring, handoff, verification, or closeout point defined by the
restaurant.

• Current permits, event requirements, and approved operating plan

• Mobile-unit or booth opening/closing checklist

• Potable-water, wastewater, fuel, power, sanitation, and temperature records as applicable

• Approved food containers, labels, thermometers, sanitizer, and handwashing supplies

• Commissary loading/return log and emergency contacts

• Operate only within the scope approved by the applicable regulatory authority and property/event rules.

• Potable water, wastewater, handwashing, warewashing, power, ventilation, and food-temperature controls are verified before service.

• Food and single-service items remain protected during transport, setup, service, and return.

• Unsafe utility, fuel, sanitation, temperature, or structural conditions require service to stop until corrected or approved.

1. Confirm that mobile unit wastewater tank control is authorized under the current mobile/temporary operating plan, permit, event rules, and commissary
relationship.

2. Verify the assigned person in charge, route/site, service window, emergency contacts, and required records for the operating period.

3. Inspect the unit/booth and all relevant utilities—potable water, wastewater, handwashing, warewashing, refrigeration/hot holding, fuel/power,
ventilation, lighting, and waste control.

4. Load or stage only protected, labeled, approved product and supplies; maintain separation of food, chemicals, waste, personal items, and
fuel/equipment hazards.

5. Perform mobile unit wastewater tank control using the approved sequence and monitor any food temperature, water, power, sanitation, fuel, or
structural condition that can make operation unsafe.

6. Record required readings, fills, disposals, transfers, checks, or corrective actions at the restaurant-approved frequency.

7. Stop the affected activity when a critical utility, food-safety control, fuel system, sanitation control, or permit condition cannot be maintained.

8. Notify the manager/commissary/event authority of any deviation that affects service scope, product disposition, utilities, or safe operation.

9. At close or return, remove food/waste safely, clean and sanitize the unit/booth, secure fuel/power and equipment, and reconcile product and records.

10. The person in charge verifies the unit/site is safe, required records are complete, and unresolved defects are tagged for correction before next use.

■ Mobile Unit Wastewater Tank Control was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.


■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.

■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.

• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

Restaurant-specific critical limit, service target, frequency, or acceptance criterion: __________________________

Mobile Unit Propane and Fuel Safety SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for mobile unit propane and fuel safety so specialty operations do not
depend on improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Mobile-unit managers, commissary teams, temporary-event leaders, cooks, service employees, and employees assigned to mobile
food operations.

The mobile-unit or event manager owns readiness. The person in charge verifies permits, utilities, food-safety controls, equipment,
records, and safe return/closeout.

Whenever this specialty operation is performed and at each required monitoring, handoff, verification, or closeout point defined by the
restaurant.

• Current permits, event requirements, and approved operating plan

• Mobile-unit or booth opening/closing checklist

• Potable-water, wastewater, fuel, power, sanitation, and temperature records as applicable

• Approved food containers, labels, thermometers, sanitizer, and handwashing supplies

• Commissary loading/return log and emergency contacts

• Manufacturer-approved fuel/gas equipment and required leak/safety controls

• Operate only within the scope approved by the applicable regulatory authority and property/event rules.

• Potable water, wastewater, handwashing, warewashing, power, ventilation, and food-temperature controls are verified before service.

• Food and single-service items remain protected during transport, setup, service, and return.

• Unsafe utility, fuel, sanitation, temperature, or structural conditions require service to stop until corrected or approved.

• Gas/fuel cylinders and systems are secured, ventilated, and handled only under manufacturer, fire-code, and trained-person requirements applicable to
the location.

1. Confirm that mobile unit propane and fuel safety is authorized under the current mobile/temporary operating plan, permit, event rules, and commissary
relationship.

2. Verify the assigned person in charge, route/site, service window, emergency contacts, and required records for the operating period.

3. Inspect the unit/booth and all relevant utilities—potable water, wastewater, handwashing, warewashing, refrigeration/hot holding, fuel/power,
ventilation, lighting, and waste control.

4. Load or stage only protected, labeled, approved product and supplies; maintain separation of food, chemicals, waste, personal items, and
fuel/equipment hazards.

5. Perform mobile unit propane and fuel safety using the approved sequence and monitor any food temperature, water, power, sanitation, fuel, or
structural condition that can make operation unsafe.

6. Record required readings, fills, disposals, transfers, checks, or corrective actions at the restaurant-approved frequency.

7. Stop the affected activity when a critical utility, food-safety control, fuel system, sanitation control, or permit condition cannot be maintained.

8. Notify the manager/commissary/event authority of any deviation that affects service scope, product disposition, utilities, or safe operation.

9. At close or return, remove food/waste safely, clean and sanitize the unit/booth, secure fuel/power and equipment, and reconcile product and records.

10. The person in charge verifies the unit/site is safe, required records are complete, and unresolved defects are tagged for correction before next use.


■ Mobile Unit Propane and Fuel Safety was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.

■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

V3-021 | Drive-Thru, Curbside & High-Volume Off-Premise

Drive-Thru Lane Opening SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for drive-thru lane opening so specialty operations do not depend on
improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Drive-thru, curbside, expo, cashier, runner, kitchen, and manager teams assigned to high-volume off-premise service.

The shift leader owns lane, staging, accuracy, and recovery controls. Assigned employees execute the station process and escalate
safety, payment, timing, or accuracy failures.

Before the applicable operation, service period, event, route, batch, opening, or equipment use, and again after any material condition
changes.

• Current off-premise menu and availability list

• Order display, headset, curbside, or communication equipment

• Order labels, staging identifiers, bags, seals, and condiments

• Speed, accuracy, remake, and escalation records

• Traffic and employee-safety equipment required by the site

• Guest and employee safety takes priority over speed.

• Orders are positively identified before payment, staging, or handoff.

• Food quality, packaging, temperature protection, and order accuracy are verified before release.

• Queue controls and recovery decisions are used before volume overwhelms production or creates unsafe staging.

1. Confirm the station, lane, staging zone, menu availability, staffing, devices, and safety controls needed for drive-thru lane opening.

2. Test communications, order display/payment tools, labels, staging identifiers, and backup methods before volume builds.

3. Define who owns order acceptance, production, accuracy, payment, staging, handoff, guest updates, and recovery during the shift.

4. Keep guest/order identity attached to the order through every handoff and separate orders so bags, drinks, condiments, and special requests cannot
be mixed.

5. Execute drive-thru lane opening while monitoring queue length, order age, food quality, traffic/employee safety, and production capacity.

6. Verify order identity, required packaging, product condition, payment status, and final accuracy before release.

7. Use the approved throttle, hold, menu-reduction, recovery, remake, or manager-escalation method before delays create uncontrolled errors.

8. Communicate realistic updates to guests/drivers and document significant delays, complaints, unsafe conditions, or recurring accuracy failures.

9. Reset the staging/traffic area, clear abandoned or unresolved orders through the approved disposition process, and reconcile exceptions.

10. The shift leader reviews speed, accuracy, recovery, and safety evidence and assigns follow-up for recurring bottlenecks.

■ Drive-Thru Lane Opening was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.

■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.


• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

Applicable permit, regulatory, certification, manufacturer, event, or company requirement: ______________________

V3-022 | Drive-Thru, Curbside & High-Volume Off-Premise

Drive-Thru Order Taking and Confirmation SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for drive-thru order taking and confirmation so specialty operations do not
depend on improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Drive-thru, curbside, expo, cashier, runner, kitchen, and manager teams assigned to high-volume off-premise service.

The shift leader owns lane, staging, accuracy, and recovery controls. Assigned employees execute the station process and escalate
safety, payment, timing, or accuracy failures.

Whenever this specialty operation is performed and at each required monitoring, handoff, verification, or closeout point defined by the
restaurant.

• Current off-premise menu and availability list

• Order display, headset, curbside, or communication equipment

• Order labels, staging identifiers, bags, seals, and condiments

• Speed, accuracy, remake, and escalation records

• Traffic and employee-safety equipment required by the site

• Guest and employee safety takes priority over speed.

• Orders are positively identified before payment, staging, or handoff.

• Food quality, packaging, temperature protection, and order accuracy are verified before release.

• Queue controls and recovery decisions are used before volume overwhelms production or creates unsafe staging.

1. Confirm the station, lane, staging zone, menu availability, staffing, devices, and safety controls needed for drive-thru order taking and confirmation.

2. Test communications, order display/payment tools, labels, staging identifiers, and backup methods before volume builds.

3. Define who owns order acceptance, production, accuracy, payment, staging, handoff, guest updates, and recovery during the shift.

4. Keep guest/order identity attached to the order through every handoff and separate orders so bags, drinks, condiments, and special requests cannot
be mixed.

5. Execute drive-thru order taking and confirmation while monitoring queue length, order age, food quality, traffic/employee safety, and production
capacity.

6. Verify order identity, required packaging, product condition, payment status, and final accuracy before release.

7. Use the approved throttle, hold, menu-reduction, recovery, remake, or manager-escalation method before delays create uncontrolled errors.

8. Communicate realistic updates to guests/drivers and document significant delays, complaints, unsafe conditions, or recurring accuracy failures.

9. Reset the staging/traffic area, clear abandoned or unresolved orders through the approved disposition process, and reconcile exceptions.

10. The shift leader reviews speed, accuracy, recovery, and safety evidence and assigns follow-up for recurring bottlenecks.

■ Drive-Thru Order Taking and Confirmation was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.

■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.


• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

Applicable permit, regulatory, certification, manufacturer, event, or company requirement: ______________________

Restaurant-specific critical limit, service target, frequency, or acceptance criterion: __________________________

Record future changes in the restaurant master SOP index, retain required evidence, and retrain affected employees whenever the approved process,

7. Use the approved throttle, hold, menu-reduction, recovery, remake, or manager-escalation method before delays create uncontrolled errors.

8. Communicate realistic updates to guests/drivers and document significant delays, complaints, unsafe conditions, or recurring accuracy failures.

9. Reset the staging/traffic area, clear abandoned or unresolved orders through the approved disposition process, and reconcile exceptions.

10. The shift leader reviews speed, accuracy, recovery, and safety evidence and assigns follow-up for recurring bottlenecks.

■ Large Digital Order Queue Throttling was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.

■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.


• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

V3-031 | Commissary, Central Production & Multi-Unit Transfers

Commissary Production Scheduling SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for commissary production scheduling so specialty operations do not
depend on improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Commissary leaders, production teams, receiving teams, drivers, inventory owners, and managers at sending and receiving units.

The sending location owns production, labeling, and release. The receiving location verifies condition, identity, quantity, traceability,
and acceptance before use.

Whenever this specialty operation is performed and at each required monitoring, handoff, verification, or closeout point defined by the
restaurant.

• Approved production/transfer order

• Batch, lot, date, label, and traceability materials

• Calibrated thermometers and approved transport equipment

• Transfer manifests, receiving records, and variance forms

• Clean food-grade totes, racks, or containers appropriate to the product

• Every transferred product is identifiable from production through receipt and use.

• Time/temperature-controlled food remains within the restaurant’s approved food-safety controls during production, cooling, transport, and receipt.

• Sending and receiving quantities are reconciled and variances are documented.

• Products with compromised identity, packaging, temperature control, or traceability are held from use pending manager disposition.

1. Confirm the approved production or transfer request for commissary production scheduling, including item identity, quantity, destination, required
condition, and accountable sender/receiver.

2. Verify source product, batch/lot/date information, packaging, labels, transport containers, and calibrated monitoring tools before release.

3. Prepare product using the approved recipe/process and maintain traceability through cooling, packaging, staging, and loading as applicable.

4. Label each unit/container with the identifiers required by the company and applicable food-safety/traceability program.

5. Execute commissary production scheduling using equipment and transport conditions that protect time/temperature control, package integrity, and
product identity.

6. Record departure/release condition, quantities, times, temperatures or other required verification before custody changes.

7. The receiving employee inspects identity, quantity, packaging, traceability, and product condition before acceptance and records any variance.

8. Hold and escalate any product with missing identity, damaged packaging, questionable condition, temperature deviation, or unexplained quantity
variance.

9. Reconcile accepted, rejected, returned, wasted, and missing quantities and restore/clean reusable transport equipment.

10. Sending and receiving managers close the transfer record and investigate recurring yield, loss, timing, or cold-chain problems.

■ Commissary Production Scheduling was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.


■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.

• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

Applicable permit, regulatory, certification, manufacturer, event, or company requirement: ______________________

Restaurant-specific critical limit, service target, frequency, or acceptance criterion: __________________________

Record future changes in the restaurant master SOP index, retain required evidence, and retrain affected employees whenever the approved process,

• Calibrated thermometers and approved transport equipment

• Transfer manifests, receiving records, and variance forms

• Clean food-grade totes, racks, or containers appropriate to the product

• Every transferred product is identifiable from production through receipt and use.

• Time/temperature-controlled food remains within the restaurant’s approved food-safety controls during production, cooling, transport, and receipt.

• Sending and receiving quantities are reconciled and variances are documented.

• Products with compromised identity, packaging, temperature control, or traceability are held from use pending manager disposition.

1. Confirm the approved production or transfer request for central kitchen yield reconciliation, including item identity, quantity, destination, required
condition, and accountable sender/receiver.

2. Verify source product, batch/lot/date information, packaging, labels, transport containers, and calibrated monitoring tools before release.

3. Prepare product using the approved recipe/process and maintain traceability through cooling, packaging, staging, and loading as applicable.

4. Label each unit/container with the identifiers required by the company and applicable food-safety/traceability program.

5. Execute central kitchen yield reconciliation using equipment and transport conditions that protect time/temperature control, package integrity, and
product identity.

6. Record departure/release condition, quantities, times, temperatures or other required verification before custody changes.

7. The receiving employee inspects identity, quantity, packaging, traceability, and product condition before acceptance and records any variance.

8. Hold and escalate any product with missing identity, damaged packaging, questionable condition, temperature deviation, or unexplained quantity
variance.

9. Reconcile accepted, rejected, returned, wasted, and missing quantities and restore/clean reusable transport equipment.

10. Sending and receiving managers close the transfer record and investigate recurring yield, loss, timing, or cold-chain problems.

■ Central Kitchen Yield Reconciliation was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.


■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.

• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

Applicable permit, regulatory, certification, manufacturer, event, or company requirement: ______________________

Restaurant-specific critical limit, service target, frequency, or acceptance criterion: __________________________

Record future changes in the restaurant master SOP index, retain required evidence, and retrain affected employees whenever the approved process,

and acceptance before use.

released.

• Batch, lot, date, label, and traceability materials

V3-041 | Buffet, Self-Service & Specialty Service Formats

Buffet Opening and Line Setup SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for buffet opening and line setup so specialty operations do not depend on
improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Buffet, self-service, carving/action-station, beverage, kitchen, service, and manager teams.

The assigned station employee maintains the control during service. The shift manager verifies setup, monitoring frequency, guest
protection, replenishment, and closing disposition.

Before the applicable operation, service period, event, route, batch, opening, or equipment use, and again after any material condition
changes.

• Approved station diagram and service plan

• Calibrated thermometers and station monitoring log

• Clean utensils, pans, labels, barriers, and service equipment

• Approved sanitizer and cleaning supplies

• Replenishment, discard, and corrective-action records

• Guest self-service food is protected from contamination and monitored throughout service.

• Food is replenished in controlled quantities rather than mixing uncontrolled old and new product.

• Utensils, barriers, temperatures, labels, and station condition are checked at the restaurant’s approved frequency.

• Questionable or contaminated product is removed immediately and never returned to service.

1. Confirm the approved product/service specification and assigned employee for buffet opening and line setup.

2. Review required labels, ingredients, equipment, monitoring tools, service/display limits, rotation method, and corrective-action rules.

3. Inspect and clean the station/equipment; verify barriers, utensils, containers, monitoring devices, and guest-protection controls are ready.

4. Stage product in controlled quantities, maintain required segregation, and attach identity/date/allergen or handling information before display/use.

5. Perform buffet opening and line setup using the approved operating sequence; do not mix uncontrolled old and new product or bypass required
monitoring.

6. Check product condition, time/temperature, labels, equipment, guest-contact risk, and sanitation at the restaurant-approved frequency and record
results.

7. Remove, hold, discard, or replace product immediately when a required control is missed, contamination is suspected, or identity cannot be verified.

8. Replenish only from approved protected stock and keep the station orderly so employees can see age, condition, and ownership of product.

9. At close/changeover, dispose or store product only under the approved rule, clean and sanitize the station/equipment, and complete records.

10. The manager reviews the monitoring record and recurring deviations before the next service period.

■ Buffet Opening and Line Setup was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.

■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.


• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

Restaurant-specific critical limit, service target, frequency, or acceptance criterion: __________________________

V3-048 | Buffet, Self-Service & Specialty Service Formats

Carving Station Service and Hot Holding SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for carving station service and hot holding so specialty operations do not
depend on improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Buffet, self-service, carving/action-station, beverage, kitchen, service, and manager teams.

The assigned station employee maintains the control during service. The shift manager verifies setup, monitoring frequency, guest
protection, replenishment, and closing disposition.

Whenever this specialty operation is performed and at each required monitoring, handoff, verification, or closeout point defined by the
restaurant.

• Approved station diagram and service plan

• Calibrated thermometers and station monitoring log

• Clean utensils, pans, labels, barriers, and service equipment

• Approved sanitizer and cleaning supplies

• Replenishment, discard, and corrective-action records

• Calibrated probe thermometer and approved hot-holding/transport equipment

• Guest self-service food is protected from contamination and monitored throughout service.

• Food is replenished in controlled quantities rather than mixing uncontrolled old and new product.

• Utensils, barriers, temperatures, labels, and station condition are checked at the restaurant’s approved frequency.

• Questionable or contaminated product is removed immediately and never returned to service.

1. Confirm the approved product/service specification and assigned employee for carving station service and hot holding.

2. Review required labels, ingredients, equipment, monitoring tools, service/display limits, rotation method, and corrective-action rules.

3. Inspect and clean the station/equipment; verify barriers, utensils, containers, monitoring devices, and guest-protection controls are ready.

4. Stage product in controlled quantities, maintain required segregation, and attach identity/date/allergen or handling information before display/use.

5. Perform carving station service and hot holding using the approved operating sequence; do not mix uncontrolled old and new product or bypass
required monitoring.

6. Check product condition, time/temperature, labels, equipment, guest-contact risk, and sanitation at the restaurant-approved frequency and record
results.

7. Remove, hold, discard, or replace product immediately when a required control is missed, contamination is suspected, or identity cannot be verified.

8. Replenish only from approved protected stock and keep the station orderly so employees can see age, condition, and ownership of product.

9. At close/changeover, dispose or store product only under the approved rule, clean and sanitize the station/equipment, and complete records.

10. The manager reviews the monitoring record and recurring deviations before the next service period.

■ Carving Station Service and Hot Holding was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.


■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.

• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

Restaurant-specific critical limit, service target, frequency, or acceptance criterion: __________________________

V3-051 | Advanced Food Safety & Specialized Processes

Reduced Oxygen Packaging Approval and Control SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for reduced oxygen packaging approval and control so specialty
operations do not depend on improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Executive chefs, kitchen managers, food-safety managers, trained employees, and permit holders authorized to perform specialized
food processes.

The permit holder or designated food-safety leader owns the approved process. Only specifically trained employees may execute it,
and the person in charge verifies records and deviation response.

For every applicable batch, product, process, claim, or service event and whenever required records, approvals, or controls change.

• Current regulatory approval, variance, HACCP plan, or approved process documentation as applicable

• Calibrated monitoring instruments appropriate to the process

• Approved ingredients, packaging, labels, and process equipment

• Batch/process monitoring log and corrective-action record

• Product hold, disposition, and traceability materials

• Do not use a specialized process unless required regulatory approvals, plans, equipment, training, and records are in place.

• The restaurant’s approved critical limits and monitoring method control the process; generic internet guidance does not replace the approved plan.

• Any missed critical limit, missing record, equipment failure, or unexplained deviation triggers immediate product hold and manager review.

• Affected product is not released until disposition is authorized under the approved plan and applicable regulatory requirements.

• This process may require a HACCP plan, variance, special process approval, or defined critical limits; the current approved plan governs and must be
available to the person in charge.

1. Do not begin reduced-oxygen packaging until the person in charge verifies the restaurant has the required approved process/HACCP documentation,
equipment, labels, monitoring tools, trained employees, and regulatory authorization applicable to the food and method.

2. Review the exact approved process, critical limits/acceptance criteria, monitoring method, corrective actions, record forms, and product-disposition
rules before starting.

3. Verify approved source/ingredient identity, equipment condition, calibration, labels, dedicated tools/areas, and required segregation.

4. Start the batch/product/service record before processing so identity and required evidence remain linked from beginning to final disposition.

5. Perform reduced oxygen packaging approval and control exactly under the approved procedure; do not substitute an internet guideline or memory for
the controlled process.

6. Monitor each required critical limit or acceptance point at the specified frequency and record the actual result, employee, date/time, and lot/batch
identity as applicable.

7. Immediately stop, hold, segregate, and label affected product when a critical limit, approval, source record, certification control, or required monitoring
step is missed.

8. Notify the person in charge and follow the approved corrective-action/disposition process; obtain regulatory, program, or technical direction when
required.

9. Complete labeling, storage, service/consumer communication, traceability, and record-retention controls before affected product is released.

10. The accountable program owner reviews the complete record and authorizes release/continued use only when the documented process meets the
approved standard.


■ Reduced Oxygen Packaging Approval and Control was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.

■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.

• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

Applicable permit, regulatory, certification, manufacturer, event, or company requirement: ______________________

Restaurant-specific critical limit, service target, frequency, or acceptance criterion: __________________________

V3-056 | Advanced Food Safety & Specialized Processes

Regulatory Variance Process Control SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for regulatory variance process control so specialty operations do not
depend on improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Executive chefs, kitchen managers, food-safety managers, trained employees, and permit holders authorized to perform specialized
food processes.

The permit holder or designated food-safety leader owns the approved process. Only specifically trained employees may execute it,
and the person in charge verifies records and deviation response.

For every applicable batch, product, process, claim, or service event and whenever required records, approvals, or controls change.

• Current regulatory approval, variance, HACCP plan, or approved process documentation as applicable

• Calibrated monitoring instruments appropriate to the process

• Approved ingredients, packaging, labels, and process equipment

• Batch/process monitoring log and corrective-action record

• Product hold, disposition, and traceability materials

• Do not use a specialized process unless required regulatory approvals, plans, equipment, training, and records are in place.

• The restaurant’s approved critical limits and monitoring method control the process; generic internet guidance does not replace the approved plan.

• Any missed critical limit, missing record, equipment failure, or unexplained deviation triggers immediate product hold and manager review.

• Affected product is not released until disposition is authorized under the approved plan and applicable regulatory requirements.

• This process may require a HACCP plan, variance, special process approval, or defined critical limits; the current approved plan governs and must be
available to the person in charge.

1. Confirm that regulatory variance process control is permitted under the restaurant’s current regulatory/certification program and that all required
approvals, plans, supplier evidence, and trained personnel are current.

2. Review the exact approved process, critical limits/acceptance criteria, monitoring method, corrective actions, record forms, and product-disposition
rules before starting.

3. Verify approved source/ingredient identity, equipment condition, calibration, labels, dedicated tools/areas, and required segregation.

4. Start the batch/product/service record before processing so identity and required evidence remain linked from beginning to final disposition.

5. Perform regulatory variance process control exactly under the approved procedure; do not substitute an internet guideline or memory for the controlled
process.

6. Monitor each required critical limit or acceptance point at the specified frequency and record the actual result, employee, date/time, and lot/batch
identity as applicable.

7. Immediately stop, hold, segregate, and label affected product when a critical limit, approval, source record, certification control, or required monitoring
step is missed.

8. Notify the person in charge and follow the approved corrective-action/disposition process; obtain regulatory, program, or technical direction when
required.

9. Complete labeling, storage, service/consumer communication, traceability, and record-retention controls before affected product is released.

10. The accountable program owner reviews the complete record and authorizes release/continued use only when the documented process meets the
approved standard.


■ Regulatory Variance Process Control was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.

■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.

• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

Restaurant-specific critical limit, service target, frequency, or acceptance criterion: __________________________

any missing documentation, temperature, labeling, or source issue.

• Raw or undercooked service follows the restaurant’s current regulatory requirements and approved consumer communication.

• Specialized controls such as shellfish identity, parasite destruction, acidification, or warning labels are verified before service.

• Missing source records, identity, process evidence, or required warnings result in product hold until resolved.

• Shellfish identity and source records remain linked to the product for the period required by the applicable regulatory authority.

1. Accept molluscan shellfish only from an approved source with required source identity documentation/tag information intact and linked to the received
lot.

V3-064 | Seafood, Raw/Undercooked & High-Risk Ingredients

Sushi Rice Acidification Monitoring SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for sushi rice acidification monitoring so specialty operations do not
depend on improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Receiving employees, chefs, sushi/raw-bar teams, food-safety leaders, servers as applicable, and managers responsible for high-risk
ingredients.

The food-safety or kitchen leader owns source, identity, process, and record controls. Employees verify assigned controls and escalate
any missing documentation, temperature, labeling, or source issue.

For every applicable batch, product, process, claim, or service event and whenever required records, approvals, or controls change.

• Approved-source documentation, invoices, tags, or certificates as applicable

• Calibrated thermometers and process-monitoring tools

• Required consumer advisory, label, or traceability materials

• Dedicated containers, labels, and segregation tools where required

• Hold/disposition log and manager verification record

• Use only approved sources and maintain required identity/traceability records for the applicable product.

• Raw or undercooked service follows the restaurant’s current regulatory requirements and approved consumer communication.

• Specialized controls such as shellfish identity, parasite destruction, acidification, or warning labels are verified before service.

• Missing source records, identity, process evidence, or required warnings result in product hold until resolved.

• This process may require a HACCP plan, variance, special process approval, or defined critical limits; the current approved plan governs and must be
available to the person in charge.

1. Do not treat acidified sushi rice as controlled by pH unless the restaurant is following its current approved process, specified formulation, monitoring
method, trained-employee procedure, and any required variance/HACCP documentation.

2. Review the exact approved process, critical limits/acceptance criteria, monitoring method, corrective actions, record forms, and product-disposition
rules before starting.

3. Verify approved source/ingredient identity, equipment condition, calibration, labels, dedicated tools/areas, and required segregation.

4. Start the batch/product/service record before processing so identity and required evidence remain linked from beginning to final disposition.

5. Perform sushi rice acidification monitoring exactly under the approved procedure; do not substitute an internet guideline or memory for the controlled
process.

6. Monitor each required critical limit or acceptance point at the specified frequency and record the actual result, employee, date/time, and lot/batch
identity as applicable.

7. Immediately stop, hold, segregate, and label affected product when a critical limit, approval, source record, certification control, or required monitoring
step is missed.

8. Notify the person in charge and follow the approved corrective-action/disposition process; obtain regulatory, program, or technical direction when
required.

9. Complete labeling, storage, service/consumer communication, traceability, and record-retention controls before affected product is released.

10. The accountable program owner reviews the complete record and authorizes release/continued use only when the documented process meets the
approved standard.

V3-071 | Third-Party Delivery, Ghost Kitchens & Virtual Brands

Third-Party Delivery Platform Opening Check SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for third-party delivery platform opening check so specialty operations do
not depend on improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Expo, to-go, kitchen, digital-order, delivery, virtual-brand, and manager teams.

The off-premise leader owns platform readiness, brand separation, packaging, and release. The manager handles outages, driver
disputes, missing orders, and unresolved marketplace issues.

Before the applicable operation, service period, event, route, batch, opening, or equipment use, and again after any material condition
changes.

• Current marketplace tablets/accounts and approved menus

• Order labels, packaging, seals, and staging identifiers

• Driver/order verification method

• Marketplace outage and escalation contacts

• Refund, remake, delay, and incident records

• Only current, approved menus, prices, modifiers, hours, and availability are published.

• Orders remain separated by brand, guest, and delivery platform through production and release.

• Orders are released only after order and driver/pickup identity are verified by the approved method.

• Marketplace outages, delayed pickups, missing drivers, refunds, and chain-of-custody concerns are documented and escalated.

1. Confirm current platform/brand status, menu availability, hours, prices, modifiers, access, equipment, and owner for third-party delivery platform
opening check.

2. Test required tablets, printers, displays, network connections, labels, packaging, and backup communication before accepting affected orders.

3. Verify brand/order separation and assign clear ownership for acceptance, production, staging, driver/pickup verification, guest contact, and recovery.

4. Keep each order linked to its platform, brand, guest/order identifier, promised time, special instructions, and payment status.

5. Execute third-party delivery platform opening check using the approved chain-of-custody and handoff process; protect completed orders from
unauthorized access or mix-ups.

6. Monitor order age, platform status, driver arrival, packaging integrity, product condition, and unresolved exceptions.

7. Escalate outage, duplicate/missing orders, delayed pickup, suspected fraud, driver identity concerns, or cross-brand mix-ups before release.

8. Use the approved guest/marketplace recovery path for refunds, remakes, cancellations, and delay communication and document material losses.

9. Reconcile unresolved orders and reset availability, devices, staging, and access controls at shift end or after recovery.

10. The manager reviews recurring platform failures, delays, refunds, and handoff exceptions and assigns system/vendor follow-up.

■ Third-Party Delivery Platform Opening Check was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.

■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.


• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

V3-072 | Third-Party Delivery, Ghost Kitchens & Virtual Brands

Delivery Marketplace Order Acceptance SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for delivery marketplace order acceptance so specialty operations do not
depend on improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Expo, to-go, kitchen, digital-order, delivery, virtual-brand, and manager teams.

The off-premise leader owns platform readiness, brand separation, packaging, and release. The manager handles outages, driver
disputes, missing orders, and unresolved marketplace issues.

Whenever this specialty operation is performed and at each required monitoring, handoff, verification, or closeout point defined by the
restaurant.

• Current marketplace tablets/accounts and approved menus

• Order labels, packaging, seals, and staging identifiers

• Driver/order verification method

• Marketplace outage and escalation contacts

• Refund, remake, delay, and incident records

• Only current, approved menus, prices, modifiers, hours, and availability are published.

• Orders remain separated by brand, guest, and delivery platform through production and release.

• Orders are released only after order and driver/pickup identity are verified by the approved method.

• Marketplace outages, delayed pickups, missing drivers, refunds, and chain-of-custody concerns are documented and escalated.

1. Confirm current platform/brand status, menu availability, hours, prices, modifiers, access, equipment, and owner for delivery marketplace order
acceptance.

2. Test required tablets, printers, displays, network connections, labels, packaging, and backup communication before accepting affected orders.

3. Verify brand/order separation and assign clear ownership for acceptance, production, staging, driver/pickup verification, guest contact, and recovery.

4. Keep each order linked to its platform, brand, guest/order identifier, promised time, special instructions, and payment status.

5. Execute delivery marketplace order acceptance using the approved chain-of-custody and handoff process; protect completed orders from
unauthorized access or mix-ups.

6. Monitor order age, platform status, driver arrival, packaging integrity, product condition, and unresolved exceptions.

7. Escalate outage, duplicate/missing orders, delayed pickup, suspected fraud, driver identity concerns, or cross-brand mix-ups before release.

8. Use the approved guest/marketplace recovery path for refunds, remakes, cancellations, and delay communication and document material losses.

9. Reconcile unresolved orders and reset availability, devices, staging, and access controls at shift end or after recovery.

10. The manager reviews recurring platform failures, delays, refunds, and handoff exceptions and assigns system/vendor follow-up.

■ Delivery Marketplace Order Acceptance was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.

■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.


• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

• Refund, remake, delay, and incident records

• Only current, approved menus, prices, modifiers, hours, and availability are published.

• Orders remain separated by brand, guest, and delivery platform through production and release.

• Orders are released only after order and driver/pickup identity are verified by the approved method.

• Marketplace outages, delayed pickups, missing drivers, refunds, and chain-of-custody concerns are documented and escalated.

1. Confirm current platform/brand status, menu availability, hours, prices, modifiers, access, equipment, and owner for delivery marketplace outage
recovery.

2. Test required tablets, printers, displays, network connections, labels, packaging, and backup communication before accepting affected orders.

3. Verify brand/order separation and assign clear ownership for acceptance, production, staging, driver/pickup verification, guest contact, and recovery.

4. Keep each order linked to its platform, brand, guest/order identifier, promised time, special instructions, and payment status.

5. Execute delivery marketplace outage recovery using the approved chain-of-custody and handoff process; protect completed orders from unauthorized
access or mix-ups.

6. Monitor order age, platform status, driver arrival, packaging integrity, product condition, and unresolved exceptions.

7. Escalate outage, duplicate/missing orders, delayed pickup, suspected fraud, driver identity concerns, or cross-brand mix-ups before release.

8. Use the approved guest/marketplace recovery path for refunds, remakes, cancellations, and delay communication and document material losses.

9. Reconcile unresolved orders and reset availability, devices, staging, and access controls at shift end or after recovery.

10. The manager reviews recurring platform failures, delays, refunds, and handoff exceptions and assigns system/vendor follow-up.

■ Delivery Marketplace Outage Recovery was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.

■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.

■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.


• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.
