V3-163 | Shared Kitchens, Pop-Ups & Remote Service Venues

Shared Cold Storage Ownership and Label Control SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for shared cold storage ownership and label control so specialty operations
do not depend on improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Managers, shared-kitchen users, pop-up/event teams, concession teams, room-service teams, and employees operating in borrowed,
remote, or multi-tenant spaces.

The designated operation lead owns the space handoff, segregation, temporary setup, service controls, and restoration. The manager
escalates facility, co-tenant, contamination, access, or authority issues.

Whenever this specialty operation is performed and at each required monitoring, handoff, verification, or closeout point defined by the
restaurant.

• Site agreement, permit/event requirements, and approved operating plan

• Opening/closing and space-handoff checklist

• Dedicated labels, storage identifiers, smallwares, and chemical controls

• Food-safety monitoring and sanitation supplies

• Damage, contamination, incident, and site-restoration records

• Calibrated probe thermometer and approved cold-holding/transport equipment

• The team verifies what space, equipment, storage, utilities, cleaning, and access are actually under its control before operating.

• Food, allergens, chemicals, smallwares, and storage are segregated from co-tenants or unrelated operations.

• Temporary service does not begin until handwashing, sanitation, food protection, utilities, and safe guest/employee access are established.

• The site is returned in the documented condition required by the agreement, with unresolved issues handed off in writing.

1. Confirm the written scope for shared cold storage ownership and label control, including site owner, available space/equipment/utilities, operating
times, access rules, permits, and restoration obligations.

2. Walk the site before setup and document cleanliness, damage, pest evidence, utilities, storage, handwashing, warewashing, refrigeration/hot holding,
waste, lighting, and safe access.

3. Assign and label the restaurant’s food, chemicals, smallwares, equipment, cold/dry storage, and personal items so ownership remains clear in the
shared/temporary environment.

4. Establish handwashing, sanitation, allergen, food-protection, waste, security, and employee/guest-flow controls before food preparation or service
begins.

5. Perform shared cold storage ownership and label control within the approved footprint and prevent co-tenant or venue activity from contaminating,
displacing, or compromising the restaurant’s operation.

6. Monitor utilities, storage, product condition, access, co-tenant activity, and sanitation throughout service and document any shared-space incident.

7. Stop or relocate the affected activity if safe food handling, sanitation, utilities, segregation, or site security cannot be maintained.

8. At handoff/closeout, reconcile food/equipment, remove waste and chemicals, clean/sanitize assigned areas, and restore the site as required.

9. Document damage, contamination, missing property, shared-storage disputes, or facility defects and notify the site owner/manager promptly.

10. The operation lead completes the space-handoff record and blocks the next use until unresolved critical defects are corrected.

■ Shared Cold Storage Ownership and Label Control was performed under the current approved restaurant procedure.


■ Required people, equipment, utilities, product, approvals, and records were verified before release or continued operation.

■ Critical readings, identities, handoffs, exceptions, or other evidence required by this procedure were recorded.

■ Unsafe, out-of-standard, unidentified, or undocumented conditions were held, stopped, or escalated instead of informally accepted.

■ Corrective actions and product/equipment/guest/employee disposition were completed and documented when required.

• Using an old, generic, or remembered procedure instead of the current approved specialty-operation standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

• Failing to document actual conditions, readings, identities, handoffs, or deviations at the time they occur.

• Treating a vendor, platform, event organizer, sister unit, or outside agency as responsible for controls that still belong to the restaurant.

• Closing the activity without manager verification, record completion, cleanup/restoration, and follow-up ownership.

V3-170 | Advanced Regulatory, Food Defense & Certified Programs

Molluscan Shellfish Life-Support Display Tank Control SOP
To establish a controlled, repeatable, and verifiable process for molluscan shellfish life-support display tank control so specialty
operations do not depend on improvisation, undocumented exceptions, or unassigned responsibility.

Owners, food-safety leaders, certified-program coordinators, receiving teams, chefs, managers, and employees specifically trained for
the applicable claim or regulated control.

The designated program owner controls approved sources, certification/authority requirements, segregation, documentation, and
incident response. Managers prevent claims or service when required evidence cannot be verified.

For every applicable batch, product, process, claim, or service event and whenever required records, approvals, or controls change.

• Current certification, regulatory, supplier, or program requirements

• Approved supplier/ingredient documentation

• Dedicated labels, storage, utensils, equipment, or barriers as required

• Food-defense, claim-verification, or monitoring records

• Incident, hold, disposition, and escalation contacts

• Required shellfish source tags, invoices, labels, and identity records

• Do not make a regulated or certified claim unless the restaurant can meet and document the applicable program requirements.

• Identity, approved source, segregation, equipment, handling, and records are maintained to the level required by the specific program or authority.

• Suspected intentional tampering triggers immediate protection of people, product isolation, preservation of evidence, and management/authority
escalation.

• Any loss of certification evidence, required segregation, source identity, or control results in hold or removal of the claim until verified.

• Shellfish identity and source records remain linked to the product for the period required by the applicable regulatory authority.

1. Confirm that molluscan shellfish life-support display tank control is permitted under the restaurant’s current regulatory/certification program and that all
required approvals, plans, supplier evidence, and trained personnel are current.

2. Review the exact approved process, critical limits/acceptance criteria, monitoring method, corrective actions, record forms, and product-disposition
rules before starting.

3. Verify approved source/ingredient identity, equipment condition, calibration, labels, dedicated tools/areas, and required segregation.

4. Start the batch/product/service record before processing so identity and required evidence remain linked from beginning to final disposition.

5. Perform molluscan shellfish life-support display tank control exactly under the approved procedure; do not substitute an internet guideline or memory
for the controlled process.

6. Monitor each required critical limit or acceptance point at the specified frequency and record the actual result, employee, date/time, and lot/batch
identity as applicable.

7. Immediately stop, hold, segregate, and label affected product when a critical limit, approval, source record, certification control, or required monitoring
step is missed.

8. Notify the person in charge and follow the approved corrective-action/disposition process; obtain regulatory, program, or technical direction when
required.

9. Complete labeling, storage, service/consumer communication, traceability, and record-retention controls before affected product is released.

10. The accountable program owner reviews the complete record and authorizes release/continued use only when the documented process meets the
approved standard.

• Starting before required permits, approvals, trained people, utilities, equipment, labels, or records are ready.

Immediately when the condition is suspected or confirmed, and continuously until the manager documents safe stabilization, recovery,
or closure.
